{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Preprocess FewRel JSON into parquet format for unified pipeline.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\n\\ndef load_fewrel_split(json_path: str, pid2name: dict) -> list[dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load a FewRel JSON split and convert to flat records.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        json_path: path to train_wiki.json or val_wiki.json\\n        pid2name: {pid: relation_name} mapping\\n    Returns:\\n        list of dicts with keys: text, head_entity, tail_entity, relation_type, relation_pid\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    with open(json_path) as f:\\n        data = json.load(f)\\n\\n    records = []\\n    for pid, samples in data.items():\\n        rel_name = pid2name.get(pid, pid)\\n        for sample in samples:\\n            tokens = sample[\\\"tokens\\\"]\\n            text = \\\" \\\".join(tokens)\\n\\n            h_name = sample[\\\"h\\\"][0]\\n            h_pos = sample[\\\"h\\\"][2][0]  # list of token indices\\n            t_name = sample[\\\"t\\\"][0]\\n            t_pos = sample[\\\"t\\\"][2][0]\\n\\n            records.append({\\n                \\\"text\\\": text,\\n                \\\"head_entity\\\": h_name,\\n                \\\"head_pos\\\": json.dumps(h_pos),\\n                \\\"tail_entity\\\": t_name,\\n                \\\"tail_pos\\\": json.dumps(t_pos),\\n                \\\"relation_type\\\": rel_name,\\n                \\\"relation_pid\\\": pid,\\n            })\\n    return records\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    base_dir = Path(\\\"/root/schema-dag-extraction\\\")\\n    data_dir = base_dir / \\\"data\\\" / \\\"fewrel\\\"\\n    out_dir = base_dir / \\\"data\\\" / \\\"fewrel_parquet\\\"\\n    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\n\\n    with open(base_dir / \\\"configs\\\" / \\\"fewrel_relation_types.json\\\") as f:\\n        pid2name = json.load(f)\\n\\n    for split_name, filename in [(\\\"train\\\", \\\"train_wiki.json\\\"), (\\\"val\\\", \\\"val_wiki.json\\\")]:\\n        json_path = data_dir / filename\\n        if not json_path.exists():\\n            print(f\\\"Skipping {split_name}: {json_path} not found\\\")\\n            continue\\n\\n        records = load_fewrel_split(str(json_path), pid2name)\\n        df = pd.DataFrame(records)\\n        out_path = out_dir / f\\\"{split_name}.parquet\\\"\\n        df.to_parquet(out_path, index=False)\\n        print(f\\\"{split_name}: {len(df)} samples, {df['relation_type'].nunique()} relations -> {out_path}\\\")\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.37}}"}
```

   - **Part 1 (Count Curve)**: count={1,4,8,16,32,64,80}, 25 random batches per count, 960 test samples
   - **Part 2 (BCS-Controlled Extreme Batches)**: count=8 and count=16, 25 low-BCS + 25 high-BCS batches each
   
   The task involves: pulling data/scripts from cqa1, confirming API access works, adapting the script for local paths, dry-run, then full experiment. Output goes to `artifacts/exp_gemini_flash/`.

2. Key Technical Concepts:

   - **FewRel dataset**: 80 relations total (64 train, 16 val), 700 samples per relation. Need combined train+val for 80-relation experiments
   - **BCS (Batch Confusion Score)**: Semantic similarity between relation types. Low BCS = easier, High BCS = harder
   - **Balanced sampling**: 12 samples per relation, 80 relations = 960 total, seed=42, using `random.Random(seed).sample()`
   - **Fernet encryption**: Framework's crypto.py uses machine MAC + salt `b"agent-ml-research-tum-v1"` to encrypt secrets

   - **MCP tools**: ssh_read_only and ssh_execute for remote cqa1 access (framework-managed SSH)
   - **Data transfer challenge**: MCP tool output truncates beyond ~8000 chars; 5000-char chunks work reliably

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`/root/schema-dag-extraction/src/preprocess_fewrel.py`** (on cqa1)
     - Converts FewRel JSON to parquet format
     - Uses `configs/fewrel_relation_types.json` for PID→name mapping
     - Key function: `load_fewrel_split(json_path, pid2name)` reads train_wiki.json/val_wiki.json
     ```python
     def load_fewrel_split(json_path: str, pid2name: dict) -> list[dict]:
         with open(json_path) as f:
             data = json.load(f)
         records = []
         for pid, samples in data.items():
             rel_name = pid2name.get(pid, pid)
             for sample in samples:
                 tokens = sample["tokens"]
                 text = " ".join(tokens)
                 h_name = sample["h"][0]
                 h_pos = sample["h"][2][0]
                 t_name = sample["t"][0]
                 t_pos = sample["t"][2][0]
                 records.append({
                     "text": text, "head_entity": h_name, "head_pos": json.dumps(h_pos),
                     "tail_entity": t_name, "tail_pos": json.dumps(t_pos),
                     "relation_type": rel_name, "relation_pid": pid,
                 })
         return records
     ```

   - **`/root/schema-dag-extraction/src/fewrel_utils.py`** (on cqa1)
     - Data loading and balanced sampling utilities
     - Critical function for recreating exact sampling:
     ```python
     def sample_balanced(dataset, n_per_relation, relations=None, seed=42):
         rng = random.Random(seed)
         by_rel = {}
         for s in dataset:
             by_rel.setdefault(s["relation_type"], []).append(s)
         if relations is None:
             relations = sorted(by_rel.keys())
         sampled = []
         for rel in relations:
             pool = by_rel.get(rel, [])
             k = min(n_per_relation, len(pool))
             sampled.extend(rng.sample(pool, k))
         return sampled
     ```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_18/projects/schema-dag-extraction/scripts/create_fewrel_samples.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Create balanced FewRel samples (960 total: 12 per relation, 80 relations, seed=42).\n\nReplicates the exact sampling from cqa1's fewrel_utils.sample_balanced.\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport random\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nBASE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent\nDATA_DIR = BASE / \"data\" / \"fewrel\"\nCONFIG_DIR = BASE / \"configs\"\nOUT_PATH = DATA_DIR / \"fewrel_samples_960.json\"\n\ndef load_fewrel_split(json_path, pid2name):\n    with open(json_path) as f:\n        data = json.load(f)\n    records = []\n    for pid, samples in data.items():\n        rel_name = pid2name.get(pid, pid)\n        for sample in samples:\n            tokens = sample[\"tokens\"]\n            text = \" \".join(tokens)\n            h_name = sample[\"h\"][0]\n            h_pos = sample[\"h\"][2][0]\n            t_name = sample[\"t\"][0]\n            t_pos = sample[\"t\"][2][0]\n            records.append({\n                \"text\": text,\n                \"head_entity\": h_name,\n                \"head_pos\": json.dumps(h_pos),\n                \"tail_entity\": t_name,\n                \"tail_pos\": json.dumps(t_pos),\n                \"relation_type\": rel_name,\n                \"relation_pid\": pid,\n            })\n    return records\n\ndef sample_balanced(dataset, n_per_relation, relations=None, seed=42):\n    rng = random.Random(seed)\n    by_rel = {}\n    for s in dataset:\n        by_rel.setdefault(s[\"relation_type\"], []).append(s)\n    if relations is None:\n        relations = sorted(by_rel.keys())\n    sampled = []\n    for rel in relations:\n        pool = by_rel.get(rel, [])\n        k = min(n_per_relation, len(pool))\n        sampled.extend(rng.sample(pool, k))\n    return sampled\n\ndef main():\n    with open(CONFIG_DIR / \"fewrel_relation_types.json\") as f:\n        pid2name = json.load(f)\n\n    train = load_fewrel_split(DATA_DIR / \"train_wiki.json\", pid2name)\n    val = load_fewrel_split(DATA_DIR / \"val_wiki.json\", pid2name)\n    combined = train + val\n\n    print(f\"Combined: {len(combined)} samples, {len(set(s['relation_type'] for s in combined))} relations\")\n\n    sampled = sample_balanced(combined, n_per_relation=12, seed=42)\n    rels = sorted(set(s[\"relation_type\"] for s in sampled))\n\n    output = {\n        \"meta\": {\n            \"n_samples\": len(sampled),\n            \"n_per_rel\": 12,\n            \"n_relations\": len(rels),\n            \"seed\": 42,\n        },\n        \"relations\": rels,\n        \"samples\": sampled,\n    }\n\n    with open(OUT_PATH, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(output, f, ensure_ascii=False)\n\n    print(f\"Wrote {len(sampled)} samples across {len(rels)} relations to {OUT_PATH}\")\n    print(f\"File size: {OUT_PATH.stat().st_size} bytes\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The MD5 doesn't match. Expected d3df40aca805a5cdd6b4c430b62639c0 but got 4086ee25ad2ab3e313d4636d6b84d991. The file size is also different (303494 vs 258800 bytes on cqa1).

This could be due to:
1. JSON serialization differences (spacing, key order)
2. Different ordering of records when loading from the JSON files (Python dict iteration order depends on insertion order, which depends on the JSON parser)

Let me check if the actual samples are the same by comparing in a different way. 

The file size difference of about 17% is substantial and suggests either different JSON formatting—I used `ensure_ascii=False` while the default is `ensure_ascii=True`—or different data content. But there's another possibility: the `load_fewrel_split` function iterates over `data.items()`, so the order of PIDs in the JSON dict might not be consistent.

Even though `sample_balanced` sorts relations deterministically, the order of samples within each relation group depends on how the JSON was loaded. Since `random.sample` picks from a pool based on indices, different key ordering in the JSON would cause the same seed to select different samples. The cqa1 train_wiki.json might have different key ordering or even be a different version of the data entirely.

I should test with `ensure_ascii=True` to see if that accounts for the file size difference, then verify whether the actual data on cqa1 matches the GitHub version.

Looking at the file sizes more carefully—cqa1's file is 258800 bytes while mine is 303494 bytes. That ~44KB difference could come from how Unicode characters are encoded: with `ensure_ascii=True`, they become `\uXXXX` escapes (6 bytes each), whereas with `ensure_ascii=False`, a 3-byte UTF-8 character stays as 3 bytes. But the math doesn't quite add up there.

Let me just test with `ensure_ascii=True` and compare the MD5 to see if that's the issue. typical 3-byte UTF-8 char becomes 6 bytes. So `ensure_ascii=True` would make the file LARGER for text with non-ASCII content.

But my file with `ensure_ascii=False` is already larger (303494 vs 258800). So there must be another difference - perhaps the JSON separators. The default `json.dump` uses `', '` and `': '` separators (with spaces). Maybe the cqa1 version used compact separators `(',', ':')`.

Let me try with compact separators and `ensure_ascii=True` to see if that brings the file size down. Then I'll spot-check a few samples from cqa1 to make sure the actual content matches what I'm generating.
```

{
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport json, random\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nBASE = Path('/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_18/projects/schema-dag-extraction')\nDATA_DIR = BASE / 'data' / 'fewrel'\n\nwith open(BASE / 'configs' / 'fewrel_relation_types.json') as f:\n    pid2name = json.load(f)\n\ndef load_split(json_path):\n    with open(json_path) as f:\n        data = json.load(f)\n    records = []\n    for pid, samples in data.items():\n        rel_name = pid2name.get(pid, pid)\n        for sample in samples:\n            text = ' '.join(sample['tokens'])\n            records.append({\n                'text': text,\n                'head_entity': sample['h'][0],\n                'tail_entity': sample['t'][0],\n                'relation_type': rel_name,\n                'relation_pid': pid,\n            })\n    return records\n\ntrain = load_split(DATA_DIR / 'train_wiki.json')\nval = load_split(DATA_DIR / 'val_wiki.json')\ncombined = train + val\n\ndef sample_balanced(dataset, n_per_relation, seed=42):\n    rng = random.Random(seed)\n    by_rel = {}\n    for s in dataset:\n        by_rel.setdefault(s['relation_type'], []).append(s)\n    relations = sorted(by_rel.keys())\n    sampled = []\n    for rel in relations:\n        pool = by_rel.get(rel, [])\n        k = min(n_per_relation, len(pool))\n        sampled.extend(rng.sample(pool, k))\n    return sampled\n\nsampled = sample_balanced(combined, 12, seed=42)\nrels = sorted(set(s['relation_type'] for s in sampled))\n\noutput = {\n    'meta': {'n_samples': len(sampled), 'n_per_rel': 12, 'n_relations': len(rels), 'seed': 42},\n    'relations': rels,\n    'samples': sampled,\n}\n\nout_path = DATA_DIR / 'fewrel_samples_960.json'\nwith open(out_path, 'w') as f:\n    json.dump(output, f, ensure_ascii=False)\nprint(f'Size: {out_path.stat().st_size}')\n\" && md5sum /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_18/projects/schema-dag-extraction/data/fewrel/fewrel_samples_960.json",
  "description": "Regenerate samples without head_pos/tail_pos"
}

degradation is model-agnostic by replicating Qwen3-32B experiments\\non Gemini 2.5 Flash via TUM proxy (OpenAI-compatible API).\\n\\nPart 1 (Count Curve): count={1,4,8,16,32,64,80}, 25 random batches per count\\nPart 2 (BCS-Controlled): count=8 + count=16, 25 low-BCS + 25 high-BCS batches each\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python3 scripts/run_gemini_flash_fewrel.py --mode dry_run\\n    python3 scripts/run_gemini_flash_fewrel.py --mode part1\\n    python3 scripts/run_gemini_flash_fewrel.py --mode part2\\n    python3 scripts/run_gemini_flash_fewrel.py --mode all\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport logging\\nimport os\\nimport random\\nimport sys\\nimport time\\nfrom collections import defaultdict\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nfrom typing import Dict, List, Optional\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\n\\nBASE_DIR = Path(\\\"/root/schema-dag-extraction\\\")\\nOUTPUT_DIR = BASE_DIR / \\\"artifacts\\\" / \\\"exp_gemini_flash\\\"\\n\\n# TUM proxy config (OpenAI-compatible)\\nDEFAULT_API_KEY = \\\"tum_XXB3sZILRCg8llb3NjxVbbRNzFpsYVFdiogFkavpGn8\\\"\\nDEFAULT_BASE_URL = \\\"https://api.openai.com/v1\\\"\\nDEFAULT_MODEL = \\\"gemini-2.5-flash\\\"\\n\\nPART1_COUNTS = [1, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 80]\\nN_BATCHES_PER_COUNT = 25\\nN_SAMPLES_PER_REL = 12\\nN_RELATIONS = 80\\nTOTAL_SAMPLES = N_SAMPLES_PER_REL * N_RELATIONS  # 960\\n\\n\\ndef build_prompt_re(sentence: str, head: str, tail: str,\\n                    relation_types: List[str]) -> str:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Build RE classification prompt (matches Qwen3 experiments exactly).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    candidates = \\\", \\\".join(sorted(relation_types))\\n    return (\\n        \\\"Given the following sentence and two entities, \\\"\\n        \\\"classify the relation between them.\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n        f'Sentence: \\\"{sentence}\\\"\\\\n'\\n        f'Head entity: \\\"{head}\\\"\\\\n'\\n        f'Tail entity: \\\"{tail}\\\"\\\\n\\\\n'\\n        f\\\"Candidate relations: {candidates}\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n        'Output a single JSON object: {\\\"relation\\\": \\\"&lt;relation_type>\\\"}\\\\n'\\n        'If none of the candidate relations apply, output: {\\\"relation\\\": \\\"none\\\"}'\\n    )\\n\\n\\ndef call_api_single(client, model: str, prompt: str, relation_types: List[str],\\n                    max_retries: int = 5) -> str:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Single API call with exponential backoff retry.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    valid_relations = set(relation_types + [\\\"none\\\"])\\n\\n    for attempt in range(max_retries):\\n        try:\\n            response = client.chat.completions.create(\\n                model=model,\\n                messages=[{\\\"role\\\": \\\"user\\\", \\\"content\\\": prompt}],\\n                temperature=0.0,\\n                max_tokens=64,\\n                response_format={\\\"type\\\": \\\"json_object\\\"},\\n            )\\n            text = response.choices[0].message.content.strip()\\n            parsed = json.loads(text)\\n            pred = parsed.get(\\\"relation\\\", \\\"none\\\")\\n            if pred not in valid_relations:\\n                pred = \\\"none\\\"\\n            return pred\\n        except json.JSONDecodeError:\\n            logger.warning(\\\"JSON decode error attempt %d: %s\\\", attempt + 1,\\n                           text[:100] if 'text' in dir() else \\\"N/A\\\")\\n            if attempt == max_retries - 1:\\n                return \\\"none\\\"\\n        except Exception as e:\\n            wait = min(2 max_retries - 1:\\n                time.sleep(wait)\\n            else:\\n                return \\\"none\\\"\\n    return \\\"none\\\"\\n\\n\\ndef load_fewrel_samples(seed: int = 42) -> List[Dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load 960 balanced FewRel samples (12 per relation).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    sys.path.insert(0, str(BASE_DIR / \\\"src\\\"))\\n    from fewrel_utils import load_fewrel, get_all_relation_types, sample_balanced\\n\\n    all_samples = load_fewrel()\\n    all_relations = get_all_relation_types(all_samples)\\n    samples = sample_balanced(all_samples, N_SAMPLES_PER_REL,\\n                              relations=all_relations, seed=seed)\\n    logger.info(\\\"Loaded %d samples (%d relations)\\\", len(samples), len(all_relations))\\n    return config_path)\\n    return config\\n\\n\\ndef generate_count1_batches(all_relations: List[str], n_batches: int = 25,\\n                            seed: int = 42) -> List[Dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Generate count=1 batches (each is a single relation).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rng = random.Random(seed)\\n    selected = rng.sample(all_relations, min(n_batches, len(all_relations)))\\n    if len(selected) < n_batches:\\n        selected = selected + rng.choices(all_relations, k=n_batches - len(selected))\\n    return [{\\\"types\\\": [rel], \\\"bcs\\\": 0.0} for rel in selected[:n_batches]]\\n\\n\\ndef load_extreme_batches() -> Dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load BCS-controlled extreme batches for count=\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, degradation is model-agnostic by replicating Qwen3-32B experiments\\non Gemini 2.5 Flash via TUM proxy (OpenAI-compatible API).\\n\\nPart 1 (Count Curve): count={1,4,8,16,32,64,80}, 25 random batches per count\\nPart 2 (BCS-Controlled): count=8 + count=16, 25 low-BCS + 25 high-BCS batches each\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python3 scripts/run_gemini_flash_fewrel.py --mode dry_run\\n    python3 scripts/run_gemini_flash_fewrel.py --mode part1\\n    python3 scripts/run_gemini_flash_fewrel.py --mode part2\\n    python3 scripts/run_gemini_flash_fewrel.py --mode all\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport elif \\\"_high_\\\" in bid:\\n            grouped[ck][\\\"high\\\"].append({\\\"batch_id\\\": bid, \\\"accuracy\\\": acc, \\\"bcs\\\": stats[\\\"bcs\\\"]})\\n\\n    summary = {}\\n    for ck in sorted(grouped.keys()):\\n        low_accs = [b[\\\"accuracy\\\"] for b in grouped[ck][\\\"low\\\"]]\\n        high_accs = [b[\\\"accuracy\\\"] for b in grouped[ck][\\\"high\\\"]]\\n\\n        result = {\\n            \\\"low_mean_acc\\\": float(np.mean(low_accs)) if low_accs else 0,\\n            \\\"high_mean_acc\\\": float(np.mean(high_accs)) if high_accs else 0,\\n            \\\"low_std_acc\\\": float(np.std(low_accs)) if low_accs else 0,\\n            \\\"high_std_acc\\\": float(np.std(high_accs)) if high_accs else 0,\\n            \\\"n_low\\\": len(low_accs),\\n            \\\"n_high\\\": len(high_accs),\\n            \\\"low_batches\\\": grouped[ck][\\\"low\\\"],\\n            \\\"high_batches\\\": grouped[ck][\\\"high\\\"],\\n        }\\n\\n        # Bootstrap CI for delta\\n        if low_accs and high_accs:\\n            result.update(bootstrap_delta_ci(low_accs, high_accs))\\n\\n        summary[ck] = result\\n    return summary\\n\\n\\ndef bootstrap_delta_ci(low_accs, high_accs, n_bootstrap=10000, seed=42):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Bootstrap 95% CI for mean(low) - mean(high).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\\n    low_arr = np.array(low_accs)\\n    high_arr = np.array(high_accs)\\n    observed_delta = float(low_arr.mean() - high_arr.mean())\\n\\n    deltas = []\\n    for _ in range(n_bootstrap):\\n        l_boot = rng.choice(low_arr, size=len(low_arr), replace=True)\\n        h_boot = rng.choice(high_arr, size=len(high_arr), replace=True)\\n        deltas.append(l_boot.mean() - h_boot.mean())\\n\\n    deltas = np.array(deltas)\\n    return {\\n        \\\"delta_pp\\\": round(observed_delta * 100, 2),\\n        \\\"ci_lower_pp\\\": round(float(np.percentile(deltas, 2.5)) * 100, 2),\\n        \\\"ci_upper_pp\\\": round(float(np.percentile(deltas, 97.5)) * 100, 2),\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef run_dry_run(client, model: str, samples: List[Dict]):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Quick validation: 1 batch, ~5 samples.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    logger.info(\\\"=== DRY RUN: testing API connectivity ===\\\")\\n\\n    # Pick a count_8 batch\\n    batch_config = load_count_curve_batches()\\n    test_batch = batch_config[\\\"count_8\\\"][\\\"random\\\"][0]\\n    batch_types = test_batch[\\\"types\\\"]\\n    type_set = set(batch_types)\\n\\n    test_samples = [s for s in samples if s[\\\"relation_type\\\"] in type_set][:5]\\n    if not test_samples:\\n        test_samples = samples[:5]\\n        batch_types = list(set(s[\\\"relation_type\\\"] for s in test_samples))\\n\\n    logger.info(\\\"Testing with %d samples, %d candidate types\\\", len(test_samples), len(batch_types))\\n    logger.info(\\\"Batch types: %s\\\", batch_types)\\n\\n    correct = 0\\n    for s in test_samples:\\n        prompt = build_prompt_re(s[\\\"text\\\"], s[\\\"head_entity\\\"], s[\\\"tail_entity\\\"], batch_types)\\n        t0 = time.time()\\n        pred = call_api_single(client, model, prompt, batch_types)\\n        elapsed = time.time() - t0\\n        is_correct = pred == s[\\\"relation_type\\\"]\\n        correct += int(is_correct)\\n        logger.info(\\\"  GT=%-35s PRED=%-35s %.2fs %s\\\",\\n                    s[\\\"relation_type\\\"], pred, elapsed,\\n                    \\\"OK\\\" if is_correct else \\\"MISS\\\")\\n        time.sleep(0.2)\\n\\n    logger.info(\\\"=== DRY RUN COMPLETE: %d/%d correct ===\\\", correct, len(test_samples))\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\\\"Gemini 2.5 Flash FewRel cross-model validation\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--mode\\\", choices=[\\\"dry_run\\\", \\\"part1\\\", \\\"part2\\\", \\\"all\\\"], default=\\\"all\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--api_key\\\", default=os.environ.get(\\\"GEMINI_API_KEY\\\", DEFAULT_API_KEY))\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--base_url\\\", default=os.environ.get(\\\"OPENAI_BASE_URL\\\", DEFAULT_BASE_URL))\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--model\\\", default=DEFAULT_MODEL)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--output_dir\\\", default=str(OUTPUT_DIR))\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--seed\\\", type=int, default=42)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--sleep\\\", type=float, default=0.1,\\n                        help=\\\"Sleep between API calls (seconds)\\\")\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n\\n    logging.basicConfig(\\n        level=logging.INFO,\\n        format=\\\"%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(name)s: %(message)s\\\",\\n        handlers=[\\n            logging.StreamHandler(),\\n            logging.FileHandler(Path(args.output_dir) / \\\"run.log\\\", mode=\\\"a\\\"),\\n        ]\\n    )\\n\\n    logger.info(\\\"Config: model=%s, base_url=%s, seed=%d, sleep=%.2f\\\",\\n                args.model, args.base_url, args.seed, args.sleep)\\n\\n    # Initialize OpenAI-compatible client\\n    from openai import OpenAI\\n    client = OpenAI(api_key=[REDACTED], base_url=args.base_url)\\n    logger.info(\\\"Client initialized (OpenAI-compatible, model=%s)\\\", args.model)\\n\\n    # Load data\\n    samples = load_fewrel_samples(seed=args.seed)\\n\\n    output_dir = Path(args.output_dir)\\n    output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\n\\n    if args.mode == \\\"dry_run\\\":\\n        run_dry_run(client, args.model, samples)\\n        return\\n\\n    if args.mode in (\\\"part1\\\", \\\"all\\\"):\\n        run_part1(client, args.model, samples, output_dir / \\\"part1\\\", args.sleep)\\n\\n    if args.mode in (\\\"part2\\\", \\\"all\\\"):\\n This means I can't easily regenerate the batch_config.json locally because:
1. I need the similarity matrix (a .pt file from cqa1)
2. I need the `select_type_batches.py` module


3. The file is pretty-printed with indentation, so compacting it would significantly reduce its size

Instead of trying to regenerate it, I could use the MCP tool to compress and minify the batch_config.json on cqa1, then transfer it in smaller chunks.
